Rio Ngumoha on escaping crime, leaving Chelsea and his England dream

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

At 18 years of age, wonderkid Ngumoha is the youngest player in England’s squad and tells Will Castle about his journey to realising his international football dream so early

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Rio Ngumoha discovered from an early age that he was destined for the the fast-track treatment in football and at first he didn’t exactly like it.

“I think I was six, maybe five,” recalls Ngumoha. “I was playing for a local team. I remember I got an assist and then next thing you know, I could hear my mum on the side saying well done, and then they started saying that I was playing with older players. I just panicked and I started crying and then I walked off the pitch because I was so scared because everyone was older than me.”

It took this shock to the system to understand the precocious talent that he was. “I realised after I could definitely have competed with them, so I’ve toughened up a bit.” Punching above his weight went on to become the norm for Ngumoha; it still is. At 18, he is the youngest player in the senior England squad, picked by Thomas Tuchel for what is already his third camp this month.

No longer is Ngumoha stunned by his rate of progress. It’s what the Liverpool starlet has come to expect from himself; he said last month that he “definitely had the confidence and the belief” to think he could “wear an England shirt at 18”. But such confidence didn’t come without his humble beginnings, grinding in the cages, where he would visualise himself one day representing his country. “I feel like if you don't visualise anything or you don't dream big enough, then you might not ever achieve it,” he asserts.

Made in the Manor (Park), Ngumoha grew up in a particularly rough part of east London. “Everyone knows it’s a tough area, Newham especially,” he says. There are darker avenues he could have ended up going down, but those around him – chiefly his mum Liz and his older brother James – ensured he stayed on the right track.

“I think the people around me and all my friends and family made sure I wasn't a part of anything that was too rough, or just to keep me on my toes really. And I had football, which is a top, top escape route for things like crime and stuff. So I think I'm just having my brother there, my mum, all my friends supporting me. They just knew to leave me out of all of that stuff so I can get to the top.”

James especially was integral to his footballing rise. “My brother, he's been a massive part of my journey,” Ngumoha adds. “He saw the talent in me from when I was like five years old, which is crazy to say. He’s my agent as well now. He just wants the best for me, and he's really supportive.”

There were things that family couldn’t necessarily help him with, though. His commute to and from Chelsea’s Cobham training ground, which before the days of the Elizabeth line required him to take two trains and two tubes – a journey that touched two hours – was a daunting and taxing one for a child to take on a near-daily basis.

“I think sometimes going from school to get the train is a bit tough, especially coming back late around 11pm. For a schoolkid, it isn't easy,” he reflects. “But that's a sacrifice you've got to make sometimes to reach the top.”

Chelsea were hoping that continued sacrifice would reap mutual rewards for both club and player. Academy coaches felt he could win the Ballon d’Or – à la Harry Kane, his international captain – wearing the blue shirt. But those Chelsea-centric dreams were dashed when Ngumoha made the bold decision to swap Cobham – and the reputation for youth development that comes with it – for Kirkby, joining rivals Liverpool in 2024, aged 16.

The nature of his departure upset Chelsea, and Liverpool have since been ordered to pay at least £2.8m in compensation by a tribunal. That could rise to a maximum of £6.8m with various add-ons. Either way, Liverpool are laughing. And Ngumoha has no regrets.

“I feel like I needed a new challenge,” he explains. “I feel like if I stayed at Chelsea... not saying that I wouldn't have got to the first team as quickly, because I believe in my ability, but I feel like Liverpool just gave me a new pathway.

“The plan that they set for me was straightforward really. For me, it was a no-brainer going to Liverpool, and they just said if I prove myself, because they know how much ability I had, then they know that I would definitely be a first-team player and definitely a star for them in the making.”

After a breakout season last term, Ngumoha is now a Liverpool regular under Andoni Iraola and is treasured by the fanbase, which he describes as the best in the world. “I think going to Liverpool so far has been the right choice.”

Ngumoha, the man, finds himself on a collision course for superstardom with club and country. And he could make his first England start in the coming days.

But he won’t do it without keeping those who moulded him close. His mum and brother have moved with him to Merseyside, leaving life in London behind. They will be constants in the shaping of a player who could become one of the truly great English footballers.

The Gear Rémi Bonnet Uses to Run up Mountains—and the Tudor Watch He Wears

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

Inside the shoes and layers the endurance athlete relies on for steep climbs.

RÉMI BONNET MAKES running up mountains look easy. The Swiss endurance athlete holds the world record for the vertical kilometer, having climbed more than 3,200 feet in just 27 minutes and 21 seconds. It’s the equivalent of running nearly three Empire State Buildings straight up in less than half an hour.

That's why Tudor, Rolex's adventurous younger brother and one of our favorite watch brands, tapped the 31-year-old athlete for its “Born to Dare” campaign, which celebrates high-achieving risk-takers. For Bonnet, that partnership means "daring to take risks, daring to try new things, and daring to push the limit a bit further."

When Men's Health caught up with Bonnet in Chamonix, France, during UTMB Mont-Blanc, one of the world's most prestigious trail-running events, he shared how his gear has evolved over the years. While he once obsessed over shaving every possible gram off his equipment, he's learned that lighter isn't always better, especially when it comes to performance.

We asked Bonnet to share the six pieces of gear he swears by, from his favorite trail-running shoes to a blister-prevention product.

As a Tudor ambassador, Bonnet has his pick of some seriously handsome watches, but the Ranger is his favorite. We're also big fans. Inspired by Tudor’s history of exploration, this rugged field watch features a stainless steel case, scratch-resistant sapphire crystal, and water resistance up to 100 meters, so it can take a licking. With multiple case sizes and strap options (stainless steel bracelet! Fabric strap! Rubber!), you can find one that fits your vibe, whether you're dressing up for dinner or heading outdoors.

Bonnet relies on Normatec compression boots after grueling races and long flights. The latest Elite model is completely wireless, with up to four hours of battery life, making it easy to squeeze in a leg massage whether you're at home or on the road. It also offers seven pressure settings to customize the intensity. A Men’s Health tester found the boots helped relieve his tired legs after a tough workout, and he even fell asleep during a 60-minute session.

Read more: Hyperice Normatec Elite Review

Before races, Bonnet puts on this anti-chafing cream. A blend of shea butter forms a thin, sweat-resistant barrier to help prevent blisters and friction mile after mile.

Bonnet loves these trail-running socks, which feature targeted cushioning in high-pressure areas like the heels and balls of the feet, plus moisture-wicking fabric to keep his feet dry mile after mile. They're also designed with extra support around the ankles to help lock the socks in place on uneven terrain.

As a Salomon-sponsored athlete, Bonnet is already running in the upcoming S/Lab Pulsar 5. "It is a mix between lightness and the performance of a road shoe," he says. Until that model hits shelves, the Pulsar 4 is the next best thing. It weighs just 9 ounces and features a dual-foam midsole, abrasion-resistant Matryx upper, and 3.5-millimeter rubber lugs for traction on rocky trails.

Bonnet uses an Insta360 camera to document his mountain adventures for Instagram. We're also fans of the brand, having awarded its GO Ultra a Men’s Health Tech Award in 2026. The latest X6 shoots sharp 8K video, with impressive image stabilization that keeps footage smooth even when you're shredding down a mountain. Its invisible selfie stick disappears from your footage for drone-like shots without needing someone else to film you.

He started his magazine career as an accessories assistant at Vogue, and has since held editorial roles at Tatler Asia and The Manual. When he’s not online shopping, you can find him on long walks with his two corgis.

ALMA Reveals a Jet of Cool Gas from a Microquasar

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

There's a strange gamma-ray emitter in the Large Magellanic Cloud that has astronomers stumped about its source. It lies within the ashes left behind by a supernova that created a compact object that's part of the system. The supernova remnant provides a piece of evidence to explain the emissions, but not the whole story.

So, a research team from two Japanese universities decided to give the object, called LMC P3, a closer look. Instead of tracking the gamma rays, they looked at the object with the Atacama Large Millimeter/Submillimeter Array (ALMA) in Chile. This is a series of radio telescopes that provide highly detailed radio views of objects in the sky. ALMA focused on radio emissions from carbon monoxide (CO) molecules in proximity to the cold cloud in the area around LMC P3. It's one of the few such objects found outside the Milky Way, and astronomers have dubbed it a microquasar. It consists of a giant O-type star and a compact object that could be explained by the existence of a black hole or a neutron star.

According to astronomer Yasuo Fukui of Nagoya University, who worked with team leader Bhuvana Gadikere Rajendra of Gifu University, what they detected at LMC P3 is unusual. âWe discovered a gigantic molecular jet,â said Fukui. The jet consists of molecular gas that stretches out across 26 light-years of space. For reference, the bright star Vega lies 25 light-years away from us, so the jet would connect us and that star quite easily.

The strange thing is, the molecular jet itself is cold (by our standards), but astrophysically "heated" to a temperature of about 35 to 60 Kelvin. By comparison, that's warmer than the 15 Kelvin surrounding gas but still very cold. That temperature difference means that the gas is interacting with another jet— this one a high-energy stream of particles coming from the nearby compact object with enough energy to power gamma-ray emissions. The object turns out to be a micro-quasar, which would have the energy to do the job.

Micro-quasars are interesting objects. They actually consist of two objects: a stellar-mass black hole or neutron star coupled with a companion star. The black hole constantly sucks material from the star, and that creates a swirling accretion disk around the black hole. It's a fairly energetic interaction that leads to X-ray and gamma-ray emissions. It also creates high-speed jets that travel out at nearly light-speed velocities. They're called micro-quasars because they're a smaller type of quasar, which is a highly active region around a supermassive black hole at the center of a galaxy.

Fukui said the relatively high temperature observed over a large fraction of the molecular cloud makes it a very strong case that the gas is interacting with a high-energy jet coming from the compact object â precisely the kind of jet that can also power LMC P3âs gamma rays. This also provides a boost to the idea that the object is a black hole, leftover from the collapse of a supermassive star in a supernova explosion.

Prior to this discovery, scientists had two competing theories about what could be powering the emissions. One is that pulsar winds could have been causing the emissions. That means the object has to be a pulsar, which would send out streams of energetic particles in pulsations as the object spins. Neutron stars are created when a supermassive star collapses, but a stellar remnant remains behind consisting solely of neutrons packed tightly together in a space about the size of a city.

The other theory is that the object is indeed a microquasar. The discovery of the newly found "cold" jet strongly suggests that the compact object could be a stellar-mass black hole or neutron star that draws in material from its companion to form an accretion disk. Since black holes drive more powerful jets than neutron stars, the fact that LMC P3âs gamma-ray emission is particularly high is a smoking gun pointing toward the micro-quasar origin of the signals found by ALMA. âIt provides very strong support for the possibility that its central object is a black hole," said Fukui.

Object LMC P3 was first discovered in 2016 thanks to its very strong gamma-ray emissions. It was also the first one found outside the Milky Way. From the beginning, astronomers suspected it was an X-ray binary, and Chandra X-ray Observatory studies seemed to confirm that. Then, the FERMI telescope observed it and found a 10.3-day period in gamma-ray emissions from the object. That suggested a number of ideas, including jets.

Detailed observations with ALMA focused on the surrounding gas clouds and found the "cold" jet streaming away. That raised questions about the "launch mechanism" for such a jet. In the paper the team published about their observations, they wrote: "We interpret that the high temperatures are due to shock heating of a microquasar jet driven by the gamma-ray binary, where the compact object has an accretion disk fed by the O-star winds. The CO jet matches existing predictions from magnetohydrodynamical simulations, which show that a CO jet can form from the interaction of the microquasar jet and an ambient interstellar medium (ISM) cloud. These results provide strong evidence that CO jets are a signature sculpted by microquasar jets, lending support for mass accretion in LMC P3 as the gamma-ray origin.

This discovery has important astrophysical implications for the study of high-energy emissions and the objects that provide them. Coupled with ongoing studies of such objects as SS 433, which is the first microquasar ever found, discoveries such as LMC P3 give new insight into the actions of compact objects and their interactions with surrounding objects.

Has a Mysterious Gamma-ray Powerhouse Revealed its Smoking Gun?

ALMA Observations of DEM L241/LMC P3 in the Large Magellanic Cloud: Evidence for the Formation of Cool Molecular Jets Driven by a Microquasar

Aman Confirms Its Secret Third Brand Is Atma, Opening in Miami in 2031

Photo Credit: Photo-illustration of what Atma Miami, and its Baywalk View, might look like when the project opens in 2031. Aman Group

Aman has confirmed what the paper trail suggested to Skift in January. The open question is delivery: Janu, the group's second brand, has one hotel open after more than six years since its launch.

Aman Group said Tuesday it's launching Atma, a third hotel brand built around sports, wellness, and "performance living." The first property, a hotel with branded residences and a private members club, is set to be completed in Miami in 2031.

Tennis champion Novak Djokovic is a "global partner" who will help lead the brand's focus on longevity, nutrition, and wellness programming, Aman said.

The Tuesday announcement confirms Skift's reporting in January that the ultra-luxury operator planned to open a brand under the Atma name and that the Miami project would be its first location.

Aman Group is launching a third brand, Atma, built around sports, wellness, and what it calls "performance living," with the first property — a hotel with branded residences and a private members club — set for completion in Miami in 2031. Tennis champion Novak Djokovic is a "global partner" who will help lead programming on longevity, nutrition, and wellness, Aman said. The Miami project sits in a planned 1,049-foot tower on the Brickell waterfront, with a 120,000-square-foot spa and wellness center including indoor tennis, squash, and padel courts, a thermal spa, and a medical wellness center. Residences run from one-bedroom units to six-bedroom penthouses, and owners get priority nomination to the invitation-only Atma Club. Developers are OKO Group, owned by Aman Chairman and CEO Vlad Doronin, and Oak Row Equities. Aman didn't disclose a room count, financial terms, or how it will fund the growth. The announcement confirms Skift's January reporting on the brand name and the Miami location. It also lands against a slow rollout record: second brand Janu debuted in 2020 and only Janu Tokyo has opened, while Janu Dubai has moved from a 2027 opening to 2029 on the brand's website.

Bitcoin slides below $74,000 after hotter-than-expected CPI as analysts warn of drop to $70k

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

November has historically been BTC's strongest month, with an average return of 34.4% and a median gain of 8.6% since 2010.

A hotter-than-expected U.S. inflation report sent crypto prices lower Tuesday, with bitcoin falling below $74,000 as analysts warned of a potential slide below the $70,000 level.

October's Consumer Price Index rose 0.4%, above forecasts of 0.3%, while core CPI increased 0.3% against expectations of 0.2%, raising concerns about the Federal Reserve's interest rate outlook.

"For Bitcoin, higher yields and a stronger dollar could add to recent selling pressure," said Bitget analyst Lewis Huang. "The 200-day moving average near $73,200 is a key support level to watch. A sustained move below it could increase the risk of a deeper pullback."

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) was trading at $73,363, down 1.6%. The $73,000 level is particularly important, according to Matt Mena, senior crypto research strategist at 21Shares, as it sits near bitcoin's pre-election 2024 all-time high and its April 2025 lows.

A sustained break below that level could send BTC toward $69,000, which would put the cryptocurrency near its 2021 cycle high, Mena said. He also pointed to the 50-week moving average as an important technical support level, warning that a break could put further pressure on Ethereum's $2,000 support, with $1,800 the next level to watch.

Ether (ETH) was down 3.7% to $2,042, while Solana (SOL) dropped more than 4% to trade below $83.

There is still a potential upside scenario if bitcoin can hold the $73,000 level and equities continue to rebound. 21Shares sees BTC potentially retesting $78,000, with a move above that level putting $85,000 in reach by year-end. November has historically been BTC's strongest month, with an average return of 34.4% and a median gain of 8.6% since 2010.

Beyond the immediate macroeconomic pressure, institutional participation and the growing use of derivatives could also influence bitcoin's volatility, according to Wincent senior director Paul Howard.

"As institutional participation in digital assets continues to mature, the growing use of derivatives and sophisticated hedging strategies is helping to reshape Bitcoin's volatility profile," Howard said. "Institutional investors are increasingly able to manage their exposure through options and other risk-management instruments rather than responding to every macroeconomic development by buying or selling the underlying asset."

Without a meaningful shift in monetary policy, regulatory clarity, or institutional capital flows, Howard said, bitcoin is more likely to experience a gradual downward drift than mount a sustained rally toward the $100,000 level previously anticipated.

Google Fi adds support for a second 5G network, won't say which one

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Fi has always supported T-Mobile, but the new one? Google won’t say.

Google has announced a “super network” upgrade for its Fi cell service. For the past few years, Google Fi has operated exclusively on the T-Mobile network, but now it’s adding support for a second 5G network. Oddly, Google refuses to say which one.

Google’s MVNO (mobile virtual network operator) launched in 2015 as Project Fi with support for both T-Mobile and Sprint service. The service initially only worked on Google’s Nexus phones, which would switch between networks based on signal strength. After T-Mobile absorbed Sprint, Fi switched to being a single-network service in the US while continuing to hop between networks for international travel.

With the super network, Google Fi is again a dual-carrier service stateside. Phones connected to Fi will be able to hop between T-Mobile 5G and a different 5G network to provide the best possible connectivity. Google’s blog post announcing the change conspicuously doesn’t mention either network, saying only that it supports “two of America’s best 5G cellular networks.”

We know one of them is T-Mobile, and the other must be either AT&T or Verizon. When reached for comment, a Google spokesperson declined to name the second network, which is odd. Looking at the Fi coverage map, my best guess for the super network partner is Verizon. The shape of unserved regions is a closer match than AT&T, but carriers do tend to fudge the fine details of these maps.

MVNOs don’t always advertise which network they use, and that information is getting harder to find in FAQs and support pages. Google’s Fi FAQ no longer even confirms T-Mobile as a network partner. Google used to be very upfront about its partnership with T-Mobile, pointing out that, unlike most MVNOs, it gets top prioritization on T-Mobile’s towers. Network information like this is really something consumers should have when shopping for plans—you probably know which carriers work best where you spend your time.

There are a few caveats in the super network rollout beyond the mysterious new partner. Currently, network switching is only supported on the Pixel 9, Pixel 10, and Pixel 11 series. Other devices will get access in 2027. You also won’t be able to enjoy traditional dual-SIM functionality if you want Fi to switch networks. This will only work if you have Fi configured as an eSIM with all non-Fi SIMs disabled.

If you meet those requirements, super network support is rolling out now at no additional cost on Unlimited Premium and Flexible plans.

Bilt is changing how its credit cards earn rewards, and cardholders who want to keep earning 4% Bilt Cash on everyday spending need to make a choice. New applicants must apply, be approved and accept their offer by December 31. I’ve already opted to keep the benefit—it’s a big reason my Bilt Palladium gets most of my spending.

Frequent Miler‘s latest ‘Ask Us Anything’ was a strong endorsement of Bilt. Their three Bilt Palladium Card (See rates and fees) cardholders plan to renew, and even the group’s Bilt skeptic plans to apply. They all want to keep earning 4% Bilt Cash.

If you don’t have a Bilt card, I’d get one now. You need to apply, get approved and accept the card offer by December 31, 2026 to preserve the choice of earning 4% Bilt Cash. Existing cardmembers also need to actively choose to keep it before their first card anniversary in 2027. Doing nothing means moving into the new rewards structure.

Nick Reyes calls renewing Palladium “pretty much a no-brainer.” It’s his “daily driver,” the card he uses most often. He’s happy earning two or three Bilt Points per dollar because they’re “very valuable,” with “plenty of great transfer partners” and continued good transfer bonuses. With a points accelerator active, he’ll often use it even for spending that another card would bonus. He’s approaching Platinum status (thanks to his card spend), but says the card’s earning makes sense even before transfer bonuses and status benefits.

Tim Steinke says he expects all three Palladium holders—Greg Davis-Kean, Nick and himself—to renew and retain the current rewards structure. Stephen plans to get the card before the old earning option closes, despite having no intended use for Bilt housing payments. He calls the points “absolutely fantastic.”

That’s also how I use the Bilt Palladium Card: it gets the majority of my spending. I pay my mortgage through Bilt, but the card would be valuable to me even without that.

I’ve written that Palladium looks complicated, but it’s the best everyday rewards card.

I consider these the most valuable points. You can redeem them at 1.25 cents apiece through Bilt’s travel portal, but I usually want to transfer them. The transfer partner lineup gives me access to:

Most transfers are 1:1. Accor is 3:2, I Prefer is 1:2, and Amtrak is 2:1. Bilt also runs large Rent Day transfer bonuses, often with better offers for higher elite tiers. I’ve used bonuses for transfers to Japan Airlines, British Airways, Virgin Atlantic and Air France KLM. Earning points quickly and then transferring with a bonus is why I want so much of my spending here.

Bilt is changing the earning choice in 2027. Today, cardmembers can choose:

Most cardmembers already choose the second option. Palladium is still a great card that way. But for most people putting significant spending on the card, especially when they’re generating more Bilt Cash than their housing payments would consume, I think the 4% option is better. Once you’ve reached the highest housing multiplier, spending more doesn’t increase that multiplier. With Flexible Bilt Cash, more spending keeps producing more Bilt Cash to use.

There are exceptions. I’ve explained how the housing option can produce an effective 4 points per dollar on everyday spending. At the 25% spending threshold, $1,000 of everyday spending and $4,000 of eligible housing payments earn 2,000 points from the purchases and 2,000 from housing. That’s 4,000 points against $1,000 of everyday spending. The relationship between your spending and housing payments determines which choice works best.

For me, the accelerators alone are a reason to preserve Bilt Cash earning. Spend $200 in Bilt Cash to activate one and you earn an extra point per dollar on the next $5,000 in eligible spending. Palladium’s 2X becomes 3X. That $5,000 also generates another $200 Bilt Cash, which can fund the next accelerator. Each eligible cardholder can activate five per calendar year.

My wife and I have both maxed out our accelerators this year: 3 points per dollar on $50,000 of combined spending. We’ve earned points on housing payments as well.

She has an authorized user card on my Palladium account. It costs $95 annually and comes with her own Priority Pass membership. She doesn’t receive a second set of my annual hotel and Bilt Cash credits, but she can earn rewards in her own Bilt account (or I can switch it to mine).

I’ve laid out how my wife and I use this. I directed the points and Bilt Cash from her spending into her account instead of mine. She earns her own status, funds her own accelerators, and can take advantage of transfer bonuses separately. When an offer bonuses transfers of up to 100,000 Bilt Points per member, two accounts give us room to bonus 200,000 points, with each person’s bonus determined by their own status.

An authorized user’s spending doesn’t automatically get the primary cardholder’s active accelerator. They need to activate their own, using their own Bilt Cash. Bilt also gives the primary cardholder separate card numbers and controls over authorized user spending, including transaction and monthly limits. You can add an authorized user starting at age 13. But for my wife and me, the separate rewards accounts are the reason this has been so useful.

And I’ve gotten far more out of Bilt Cash than just faster points earning. I’ve redeemed it for:

These are benefits I’ve actually used. For my everyday spending, Palladium earns the points I want faster than any other card, and the benefits have been incredible.

The new structure still allows Palladium and Obsidian cardmembers to use accelerators. Palladium’s $400 annual hotel credit and $200 annual Bilt Cash become a $600 annual Bilt Cash deposit at the card anniversary. That can fund three accelerators. Members can also earn Bilt Cash from other activity, including $50 for each 25,000 qualifying points. But they won’t replenish it at 4% of everyday spending.

Keeping the existing structure also preserves my separate $200 hotel credits, which I can stack with Bilt Cash hotel redemptions. The Bilt Cash hotel redemption allowance moves from monthly to twice yearly starting January 1 even for those keeping 4% earning, but I can still get the two $300 discounts this way.

Plus, Bilt Cash now expires at the end of the month 12 months after it’s earned. Cash earned in February 2026 lasts through February 28, 2027. The old rule would have wiped out everything over $100 at year-end. Having more time to use what I earn makes the 4% option better for me.

If you don’t have a card, I’d apply now and complete approval and acceptance before December 31. My choice is Bilt Palladium. The ability to preserve 4% Bilt Cash also applies to the Bilt Obsidian Card (See rates and fees) and no annual fee Bilt Blue Card (See rates and fees).

And if you already have a card, don’t assume your current selection of Flexible Bilt Cash protects you from the change. You need to proactively choose to keep it. I’ve already done this:

The new rewards model is selected by default. Swipe right.

You have until your first card anniversary in 2027 to make that election. Preserve it now and you can still move to the new structure later. Once that switch takes effect, you can’t move back. That’s why getting a Bilt Palladium Card makes so much sense – keeping the earning option. They’ll pry the 4% Bilt Cash earning option from my cold, dead hands, as they say.

"Making this change allows us to focus on enhancing high-quality experiences that more of our members want to use"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

PlayStation's once ambitious plans for PC seem to have softened considerably in recent months with reports suggesting Sony is moving away from porting first-party games to PC as it has done with The Last of Us, Uncharted, God of War, and more. In another shift toward console exclusivity, Sony Interactive Entertainment is now pulling the PlayStation Plus app from PC due to a low user rate. The app will no longer be available to PlayStation Plus users on PC starting December 10.

"PlayStation believes in providing high-quality experiences to our players, and we regularly review offerings to ensure we are focusing on experiences our members value most," SIE senior director of content acquisitions and operations Adam Michel says at the bottom of a PlayStation Blog announcing the latest PS Plus additions. Michel says "a very small number of eligible PlayStation Plus Premium members (less than 0.4%)" are using the PC app. "Making this change allows us to focus on enhancing high-quality experiences that more of our members want to use."

Back in May, Bloomberg reported that PlayStation's studio business group CEO Hermen Hulst had informed staff that Sony's big-budget story-driven games would no longer come to PC and instead be PlayStation console exclusives going forward. And in September, former SIE worldwide studios president Shuhei Yoshida suggested Sony could be reverting to its "older ways" of focusing on the "platform business" instead of trying to get its first-party games into as many hands as possible.

Sony itself has yet to publicly acknowledge its apparent trend away from the PC side of its games business, but just looking at how much PC ports of PlayStation games have slowed down in recent months and years, it does seem clear there's been something of a shift in focus. What that means for the next generation of PlayStation games and consoles is unclear, but another one of the company's former leaders thinks "true innovation" means a $300 PS6.

BYD and Geely approached FIA about a 12th F1 team, says Ben Sulayem

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

BYD and Geely have both approached the FIA about entering Formula 1, the sport’s governing body confirmed. FIA president Mohammed Ben Sulayem named the two Chinese automakers while speaking at the New Economy Forum in Madrid, as first reported by Grande Prêmio.

But there’s no open process for a 12th team, and neither company has a deal. The FIA is also pushing F1 toward the kind of engine an EV maker has the least reason to build.

“One of them was BYD and another was Geely,” Ben Sulayem said of the manufacturers that have reached out about joining the grid.

He said he suggested that BYD buy an existing team instead of starting a new one. BYD wasn’t interested in that route. It wants its own team, so Ben Sulayem told the company to come back with a “serious proposal” that shows the project can work.

He also made it clear that Chinese manufacturers won’t get special treatment. Any bid would go through the same process as other prospective entrants, and the rules won’t be rewritten to suit them. Right now, there’s no expansion process at all: the FIA isn’t actively looking for a 12th team.

Still, Ben Sulayem repeated that he’d like F1’s next newcomer to come from China, pointing to the country’s EV technology and the Chinese Grand Prix in Shanghai.

This isn’t new for BYD. Back in March, we reported that BYD was exploring a Formula 1 entry, either by buying a team or building one from scratch.

In April, executive vice president Stella Li said BYD had met F1 CEO Stefano Domenicali in Shanghai and that an entry was “something we’re discussing.” By July, the tone had cooled. Li said “the dream is always there, but we don’t have a concrete agenda,” and special advisor Alfredo Altavilla set a clear condition: “We will never participate in Formula 1 just to put a sticker on the side of a car.”

Altavilla said BYD would only consider F1 to the extent its technology can serve the sport.

Geely is the new name here. The group sold 3.02 million vehicles last year and owns Volvo, Polestar, and Lotus, a brand with decades of F1 history. It’s also heading into Canada next year as Chinese automakers push hard into new markets.

The last newcomer, Cadillac, took a long road. The FIA opened an expression-of-interest process in 2023, and GM’s team only joined the grid as the 11th team this season, running Ferrari power until its own power unit arrives. Audi got in the other way, by taking over Sauber.

Here’s where it gets awkward for an EV maker. The 2026 F1 power units split output roughly 50/50 between the combustion engine and electric power, with the MGU-K jumping to 350 kW.

The FIA is already walking that back. In June, it agreed to shift the split to 60-40 in favor of combustion for 2027 and 2028. And Ben Sulayem has been pushing for V8 engines running on sustainable fuel with “minor electrification” for the next rules cycle, starting in 2031 at the latest, possibly 2030. Domenicali says F1 should have a package ready to discuss by the end of this year.

So the FIA president wants a Chinese EV maker on the grid, while pushing a rulebook that makes their main strength less relevant.

We like the idea of BYD or Geely in F1. Chinese automakers have the money and the engineers, and they badly need brand prestige outside China. BYD in particular has been on a performance push with Yangwang, and F1 would be the loudest possible way to tell Europe it’s not just a cheap car brand.

But Ben Sulayem’s comments don’t move things much. There’s no entry process, BYD has already turned down his main suggestion, and Geely’s interest is a name dropped at a conference, not a bid.

A Chinese would likely be good for F1 as well. Motorsports are on the rise in China, and that would be the cherry on top in helping popularize the sport in the world’s largest market.

The bigger issue is the engine. Altavilla was clear that BYD isn’t interested in slapping a logo on a car. It wants F1 to showcase its technology, and BYD’s technology is batteries and electric drive. The FIA’s V8 push goes the other way. It’s hard to sell an EV giant on a sport that’s reducing the electric part of the powertrain.

If you’re following how fast EV tech is moving, from BYD’s batteries to F1’s hybrid power units, you can put some of that tech to work at home: pairing an EV with rooftop solar can cut your driving costs to nearly zero. With electricity rates up almost 10% last year and expected to keep climbing, going solar is one of the best ways to protect yourself against rising costs. And with lease and PPA options, you can do it with zero upfront cost and start saving immediately. If you want to find the best deal, check out EnergySage. It’s a free service with hundreds of pre-vetted installers competing for your business, so you save 20 to 30% compared to going it alone. No sales calls until you pick an installer. Florida woman arrested after targeting ex’s Pokemon cards because he wasn’t giving her attention

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

A Florida woman has been arrested after allegedly targeting her ex-boyfriend’s Pokemon card collection during an argument, before returning to his apartment for a bong and ending up facing felony charges.

Nadia Hussain, 23, was arrested in Orlando on November 7 following a bizarre confrontation involving Pokemon cards, a cellphone, and an interrupted 911 call.

According to an Orlando Police Department arrest affidavit obtained by Guessing Headlights, Hussain had spent the night at her ex-boyfriend’s apartment before the pair got into an argument.

Her ex, Jordan Bonilla, asked her to leave, but she allegedly refused, prompting him to begin recording the confrontation.

Police say Hussain knocked the phone from his hand, struck him several times, and repeatedly demanded that he “delete the video!”

She then turned her attention to his Pokemon card collection, which was sitting on the living room table.

According to WFTV, Hussain later admitted to police that she deliberately grabbed the cards after realizing how much it upset her ex-boyfriend.

She also told investigators she had been purposefully annoying him because she felt he wasn’t showing her enough affection.

Bonilla eventually escorted Hussain to her car before rushing back upstairs and locking his apartment door.

However, the encounter wasn’t over. Hussain returned, claiming she had left her bong inside.

Despite promising not to enter, she allegedly forced her way into the apartment when Bonilla opened the door to hand it over.

Police say she headed straight toward the living room before Bonilla blocked her path. She then entered a bathroom with a second entrance to the living room, leading him to believe she was attempting to reach his Pokemon cards again.

Chainsaw-wielding Florida man arrested for breaking into store and stealing $12K in Pokemon cards

Pokemon collector secretly put GPS tracker on woman’s car to find trading card restocks

Pokemon card collector sets up alleged thieves after they relist his stolen cards online

When Bonilla tried calling 911, Hussain allegedly struck his phone several times to prevent the call. Police confirmed dispatchers received a 911 hang-up from his number.

Neighbors eventually intervened and helped remove her from the apartment.

Hussain was arrested and charged with domestic battery and witness tampering to hinder communication with law enforcement, the latter being a third-degree felony.

Despite all the chaos surrounding the Pokemon collection, no theft charges involving the cards were filed.

It’s hardly the first bizarre incident involving Pokemon cards this year. In May, a Florida man was arrested after allegedly using a chainsaw to steal $12,000 worth of cards.

More recently, the FBI began investigating thieves using stolen credit cards to purchase rare Pokemon cards from trading card stores.

In Hussain’s case, there is no indication that any Pokemon cards were stolen or damaged, although the confrontation has left her facing criminal charges. The allegations have not been proven in court.

OpenAI didn't acquire Odessia for its revenue. It bought a team that built a working AI travel agent in months, plus a founder who ran a lodging company, which included hotels and short-term rentals, for a decade. It's a sign ChatGPT wants to do more in travel than send users to Booking.com and Expedia.

OpenAI has acquired Odessia, the AI travel agent startup that Sonder co-founder Francis Davidson launched just five months ago. Davidson announced the deal Tuesday and said he will join OpenAI “to focus on the future of travel.” Terms weren’t disclosed.

OpenAI has acquired Odessia, the AI travel agent startup founded by Sonder co-founder Francis Davidson, less than a year after its launch. He suggested that the support from the travelling fans was far greater, taking aim at his former rivals.

Wayne Rooney drops fresh Plymouth Argyle transfer revelation

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Former Plymouth Argyle head coach Wayne Rooney has dropped a fresh revelation on the transfer strategy at Home Park during his short tenure in charge of the club.

The England and Manchester United legend was relieved of his duties by the Pilgrims after a dismal run of form in the Championship during their eventual 2024/25 relegation campaign.

Argyle are currently in their second season in League One under his former Old Trafford teammate, Tom Cleverley, and after narrowly missing out on a spot in last season's play-offs, the Devon outfit are fifth in the table having won seven of their first 14 matches.

The mood at Home Park has returned to one of positivity after previous frustrations relating to on-pitch matters, whilst it has often been debated just how much longer Simon Hallet will be the man calling the shots from the boardroom.

Rooney was one of three hires made in the space of 12 months just two years ago, with his spells sandwiching those of Ian Foster and Miron Muslic.

As such, turnover was particularly rife and led to a period of significant instability, which Rooney has discussed with Ashley Young and Mark Goldbridge on the latest 'Stick to United' YouTube episode.

Plymouth have been told to demand up to £2m for Bim Pepple, who had been on the radar of Sheffield United, Bolton and Portsmouth in the summer.

Rooney's managerial career has been well-documented, particularly after underwhelming spells at Birmingham City and Plymouth, with both clubs eventually being relegated in successive seasons after parting company with the Liverpudlian.

The 41-year-old only oversaw one transfer window at Home Park, during which 11 players were recruited.

He has previously discussed the club's data-driven transfer policy during said timeframe, and did so again when analysing recent flaws at Old Trafford, which has led to sack calls emerging over former Middlesbrough boss, Michael Carrick.

Asked whether they can see similar traits between the recruitment issues during Ole Gunnar Solksjaer's time at the Red Devils and now by Young, Rooney related by saying: "I had it at Plymouth.

"It's all ran on data. You're looking at players, but then you get a couple of players in who you don't really know and haven't seen much of them.

"All of a sudden, you sign them and then you've got to try and make it work, but it's not what you identified," he added.

Another commented: That’s the Hallet way, I’m not a huge fan of it. I still believe Tom as Head Coach needs to ensure they fit in with how he wants them to play. I hope with Derek (Adams) in place this has changed."

"He sounds a bit unconvinced by his own point," another Pilgrims fan claimed.

Some were also previously unaware of such developments occurring, with another comment reading: "Didn't realise we did it purely off data that's strange."

Instead, they have certainly been encouraged by the form showcased by Cleverley's side in recent weeks and months, particularly after the Argyle bosses' response to last season's alarming start.

8 Metroidvanias Where the World Feels Bigger the More You Learn About It

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Eric Warner is a Journalist and Multimedia Producer based in New England with over 7 years of experience producing stories for multiple publications

One of the best aspects of video games overall is how they allow people to learn about and explore enthralling worlds in fantasy, sci-fi, or historical settings.

Many gamers would choose to play third-person open-world or narrative-focused adventure titles to explore such worlds, but there are many other game genres that allow people to delve deeper into a world’s environments and lore, such as the backtracking-centered metroidvania.

While most metroidvania games are limited to a 2D side-scrolling-like perspective, many metroidvania games can feel just as large and expansive as those found in RPGs. Especially as players continue to travel through the titles' maps and learn more about their history and inhabitants.

DrinkBox Studios' Guacamelee! is a stylish Mexican folk art and lucha libre-inspired metroidvania that saw players travel throughout the lands of the living and the dead as agave farmer turned luchador Juan Aguacate.

However, DrinkBox strove to go bigger and better with Guacamelee’s sequel, Guacamelee! 2, as that game had Aguacate travel beyond his homeland into the Mexiverse, a multiverse full of wondrous and intriguing alternate timelines.

This game almost immediately made the universe of Guacamelee far larger and more complex than most players would have imagined by just playing the first game, with Guacamelee! 2 allowing Aguacate to meet alternate versions of friends and villains as well as entirely different realms such as El Otromundo.

Konami’s Castlevania franchise is full of titles that allow players to explore and learn more about the supernatural settings and subsequent entries, as they fight vampires and other monsters. One of the more accessible entries for new players would be Castlevania: Lords of Shadow.

A third-person hack and slash metroidvania reboot of the series, Castlevania: Lords of Shadow has players embody the Brotherhood of Light knight Gabriel Belmont in 11th century Europe, as he sets out to defeat the titular Lords of Shadow and Satan himself.

However, Belmont quickly learns that there are far more dark powers hiding throughout the world as, in the Reverie and Resurrection DLCs, he travels to Carmilla's castle to defeat the ancient Forgotten One, only to become Dracula and menace humanity for hundreds of years.

From the best bosses to essential lore explanations, these are eight Metroidvanias that hide their best content behind the most unexpected paths.

Xbox consoles have become homes for many metroidvania titles over the years, with one of the earliest to debut exclusively on a Xbox console in an action-packed way being Chair Entertainment’s Shadow Complex.

Set in the same universe as Orson Scott Card's Empire book series, Shadow Complex is a military sci-fi metroidvania following Jason Fleming as he and his girlfriend, Claire, set out to explore some caves hidden in a mountain.

Things quickly go awry, though, as it’s revealed that the caves were an entrance to a labyrinth-like shadow complex run by the militant Progressive Restoration. Fleming is forced to explore the complex and steal the PR’s gear in order to rescue Clair and stop the PR from inciting a second civil war in the U.S.

One of the better metroidvanias to launch in 2026 was Drop Rate Studio’s Lovecraftian horror game DIVE or DIE - Children of Rain which takes place in the near future after most of the world was flooded by a supernatural, monster-making black rain.

Here, players must manage one of the last human settlements and routinely send people to dive into the leviathan and ruin-filled Abyssal Pool, in search of supplies and ways to appease the Cthulhu-like Rainmaker in 40 days before it destroys the world.

When players initially dive into the Abyssal Pool, they’ll first find the remains of skyscrapers and other buildings. As they continue to dive deeper and deeper below the surface, they’ll find that the Pool has almost no end in sight and is filled with all kinds of unspeakable horrors.

If you're looking for games that subvert your expectations, try these eight Metroidvanias that totally reinvent themselves halfway through.

As mentioned before, Xbox and Microsoft have supported and published many metroidvanias over the years, with some of the best they published undoubtedly being Moon Studios’ Ori and the Blind Forest and its sequel Ori and the Will of the Wisps.

The Blind Forest primarily takes places in the amazing Nibel Forest and its surrounding areas, but the first game teased other lands that were set to be explored in the Will of the Wisps.

Here, the cat-like spirit Ori and the owl Ku find themselves in the Niwen region, which has since suffered from the loss of its own Spirit Tree. This has resulted in Niwen’s inhabitants becoming corrupted and terrorizing each other, showcasing that the world of Ori is full of all kinds of magical forces and creatures.

Of all the Metroid games released over the years, the Metroid Prime subseries is arguably the most popular, with the series notably transitioning its sci-fi gameplay and maneuverability into first-person.

However, if players wanted to explore areas that feel bigger the more people learn about them, then there’s no better entry in the MP series to try out than Metroid Prime 2: Echoes.

Set roughly one year after the first MP, Metroid Prime 2: Echoes sees the bounty hunter Samus Aran travel to the planet Aether to rescue Galactic Federation Marines, only to discover that the planet is home to an advanced moth-like sentient race called the Luminoth and is at the center of a dimensional rift to an alternate hostile world called Dark Aether.

If you're looking for unexplored gems in video games, I recommend these ten Metroidvanias that are unexpected masterpieces.

Some of the more popular metroidvania series may feature vast, enthralling worlds, but sometimes metroidvanias created by small independent studios are those with the largest worlds, with one such game being Valdis Story: Abyssal City.

The second title created by Endlessfluff Games, Valdis Story: Abyssal City follows the fantasy sword-wielding warrior Wyatt and the mage Reina as they set out in search of Wyatt's father only to wind up in the sunken city of Sitheil.

From here, the two young heroes are forced to not only face off against endless waves of angels and demons but also explore and learn more about the seemingly ever-expanding Atlantis-like city of Sitheil, with the game’s ending even suggesting that other magical cities may be hidden throughout the world.

Without a doubt, the best metroidvania to play where the world feels bigger the more you learn about it is Team Cherry’s 2D masterpiece Hollow Knight.

Set primarily in the subterranean insectoid kingdom of Hallownest, Hollow Knight follows an unnamed horned Knight as they set out to explore a world that has since fallen into ruin following an outbreak of a mysterious, violence-inducing infection.

During the Knight’s travels, players will not only be able to interact with numerous insectoid inhabitants and learn more about Hallownest’s past, but also explore several subregions of the kingdom and learn about other far away lands and realms such as the City of Tears, Crystal Peak, and Dream Realm.

Looking for an exciting map to explore in the Metroidvania genre? We have the games with the best maps for you here.

Failure Is Part of Entrepreneurship. These 5 Lessons Will Help You Recover.

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

You're viewing the US website. Looking for the Site edition?

Most businesses stumble at some point. How you respond decides whether the failure becomes a setback or a turning point, and the research has some useful pointers.

Opinions expressed by Entrepreneur contributors are their own.

In 1989, I opened an Italian restaurant in Tsim Sha Tsui, Hong Kong, where the waiters sang opera arias between courses. It was a lot of fun, but it was not a good business, and it closed in 1993.

I had assumed running a restaurant was easy. It isn’t. The location was wrong, the costs were too high, and I didn’t know the trade well enough to see either problem early. Around the same time, I put money into film production and property. Both businesses struggled and were sold in the mid-1990s.

I share this because founders tend to talk about their successes and quietly bury the rest. Yet failure is a normal part of business. According to the U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics, only about half of new U.S. business establishments are still operating five years later. Hong Kong is no different. In 2025, a record number of companies registered in the city, but the South China Morning Post reported that more than 99,000 were dissolved in the same year.

Here are five lessons I have learned about failing — and about recovering.

Most of my early setbacks came from businesses I entered because they looked exciting, not because I understood them. The pattern is common. CB Insights’ 2026 analysis of startup shutdowns found that 43% cited poor product-market fit. I suspect many of those founders were very capable. They had simply misread a market they didn’t know well enough.

My rule since then has been simple: Never set up a business you don’t know. If you want to enter an unfamiliar field, partner with someone who has spent years in it, and listen to them.

The hardest decision in a struggling business is often the decision to stop. CB Insights found that 70% of failed startups ultimately ran out of capital, but running out of money is usually the last symptom, not the cause. By the time the bank balance forces your hand, months or even years may have been lost.

The same analysis, as reported by Crowdfund Insider, found that nearly a quarter of these companies had been “walking dead” for three years or more after their last funding round, still operating but no longer really growing. Hanging on rarely saves a business. More often, it makes the eventual decision more expensive.

Selling the film and property businesses was painful, but it freed up time, money and attention for something better. Closing a venture is not the same as failing as a person.

Losing a business can feel like a bereavement, and research suggests we should treat it as one. Professor Dean Shepherd’s work on grief and business failure, published in the Academy of Management Review, argues that founders recover and learn faster when they move between confronting the loss and stepping away from it, rather than dwelling on it or pretending it never happened.

The emotional cost is real. In a study of 242 entrepreneurs led by Dr. Michael Freeman of the University of California, San Francisco, 72% reported being affected by mental health conditions, either directly or through their families. Talk to someone you trust, sleep properly and keep exercising. You need a clear head to make your next decision well.

I learned to look after myself early. My father died when I was 18, aged just 49, and it made me take my health seriously long before it was fashionable. In difficult periods in business, that habit has helped keep me steady.

Experience alone does not make you a better founder. Harvard research by Paul Gompers and colleagues found that venture-backed entrepreneurs whose previous company had failed had a 20% chance of success next time, barely above the 18% for first-time founders. Those who had succeeded before had a 30% chance.

It also helps to know what kind of failure you are dealing with. In her book Right Kind of Wrong, Harvard Business School professor Amy Edmondson separates basic failures, caused by avoidable mistakes, from “intelligent failures,” which happen when you test a sensible idea in new territory and keep the downside small. The first kind should be prevented. The second is the price of progress.

The lessons don’t arrive by themselves. After a setback, I now write down what I assumed, what actually happened and what I would do differently, then ask someone outside the business to challenge it. Early in a career, a setback can feel like a judgment on your ability. With practice, it becomes a source of information.

A 2019 study in Nature by Yian Yin, Dashun Wang and colleagues analyzed more than 58,000 venture-backed startups, alongside other fields. People who eventually succeeded did not fail less often than those who didn’t. The difference was that each new attempt came faster, because they built on what they had already learned instead of starting from scratch. Speed here does not mean recklessness. It means shortening the gap between learning a lesson and acting on it.

That takes courage. The Global Entrepreneurship Monitor’s 2025/2026 report found that fear of failure deters two in five adults from starting a business. It also found that people who have previously exited a business are more likely to start again.

As I once told an interviewer, all these failures are what have shaped me. If you are recovering from one now, be patient with yourself, be honest about what went wrong, and, when you are ready, go again.

In 1989, I opened an Italian restaurant in Tsim Sha Tsui, Hong Kong, where the waiters sang opera arias between courses. It was a lot of fun, but it was not a good business, and it closed in 1993.

I had assumed running a restaurant was easy. It isn’t. The location was wrong, the costs were too high, and I didn’t know the trade well enough to see either problem early. Around the same time, I put money into film production and property. Both businesses struggled and were sold in the mid-1990s.

I share this because founders tend to talk about their successes and quietly bury the rest. Yet failure is a normal part of business. According to the U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics, only about half of new U.S. business establishments are still operating five years later. Hong Kong is no different. In 2025, a record number of companies registered in the city, but the South China Morning Post reported that more than 99,000 were dissolved in the same year.

PS Plus Pulls Plug on PC Streaming After Fewer Than 1% Used It

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Members of the manufacturer’s most expensive tier can currently stream and play titles from the PS Plus Premium catalogue using a PC app, which will be discontinued next month.

Curiously, this service has always been limited to PS4 and PS3 titles – it never expanded support to the PS5 library.

The pages on PlayStation.com where you used to be able to download the software have already been removed in anticipation.

Writing on the PS Blog, Sony’s Adam Michel said:

“PlayStation believes in providing high-quality experiences to our players, and we regularly review offerings to ensure we are focusing on experiences our members value most. With a very small number of eligible PS Plus Premium members (less than 0.4%) using the PS Plus app on PC, making this change allows us to focus on enhancing high-quality experiences that more of our members want to use. As a result, the PS Plus cloud streaming app on PC will no longer be available.”

Once upon a time, I did temporarily use PS Now streaming on my laptop when I went through a period where I didn’t have access to my consoles. But I haven’t used the feature since.

I don’t think Sony promoted this particularly prominently, so I expect many fans may only be finding out about it now.

But I think this is probably part of a broader push by the Japanese giant to move its software off PC. Internally, it’s pivoted away from porting its first-party single player games to the platform. Now it’s ceasing streaming as well.

It should be stressed that PS Plus Premium cloud streaming will remain available on the PS5, PS4, and PS Portal. There will also be no changes to Remote Play functionality.

Will you be affected by this at all? Given the numbers touted in Sony’s article, I can’t imagine many of you will have used this.

Marathon To Add New Co-Op Roguelite Mode, Social Spaces, And Other Major Features In December

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

It's beginning to look a lot like Destiny

Bungie isn't prepared to let its extraction shooter go quietly. Instead, Marathon's upcoming 'Symbiosis' update arrives on December 8, bringing a whole host of new PvE and PvP modes. Having just finished watching the new gameplay trailer above, I've got to say, It's giving off plenty of Destiny vibes.

I'm a big, big fan of Marathon, but that's not to say I don't think the game needs a shake-up. It clearly needs something to keep things ticking along. I can't help but feel extremely hyped now that Bungie has shared a first look at all the new content coming next month.

The most interesting addition of the bunch is 'Limbo': a new social space. This is where runners can hang out, chat, test weapons at the firing range and challenge each other in the duelling pit. It reminds me of The Tower from Destiny 2.

Social spaces play a huge role in fostering a sense of community in games, and Bungie knows all about that. Just look at the time Destiny players gathered at The Tower en masse to mourn the passing of Lance Reddick, the voice of Commander Zavala.

Beyond that, 'Expedition' is a new co-op PvE roguelite mode. No PvP, just you and your crew going against new threats. PlayStation.Blog shared an official description of the the new mode:

"Expedition: Phase 1 of a new PvE-only mode built around a three-run series. Play solo or with a crew, face new combat and environmental challenges without PvP threats, and use Flash Boosts to shape your build during a run."

If battling it out against other runners is more your style, Marathon is getting it's first PvP mode, Deathmatch (Beta) pitting quick 3v3 matches against one another. Personally, I think this is a great way to let players get used to the game's arsenal of weapons and gadgets without the time commitment that comes with extraction.

There are a few other new bits and bobs coming to Marathon in December, including a new runner shell, which we'll cover below:

To coincide with the Symbiosis update, Bungie will also be hosting an Open Play Week starting December 8, allowing new players to download and play Marathon for free.

The best-selling game console of all time is over 25 years old

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Sony shipped over 160 million PlayStation 2 units over its 13-year run.

You might expect newer consoles to dominate lifetime sales lists. After all, the industry is several times bigger than it was two decades ago. But all-time console sales are a blend of old and new, with Sony's second home console, the PlayStation 2, hanging onto the top spot.

However, the PS2's lead is shrinking. Based on the latest figures, Nintendo's original Switch is only a few million units behind. And unlike the PS2, it's still being sold.

A note on the figures: Sony and Nintendo report their totals as consoles shipped to retailers, which is a little different from consumer sales. Also, Microsoft doesn't publish official totals, so those numbers are analyst estimates.

Sony says the PS2 sold over 160 million units, more than any other console. How did it take the crown? For starters, it launched with a built-in DVD player, right when that market was taking off. As wild as that sounds today, this was back when physical media ruled. Come for Austin Powers, stay for Kratos.

Timing also worked in the PS2's favor. When it launched, Sega's Dreamcast was the only other new home console on the market. By the time the Dreamcast hit the US in 1999, Sony was already hyping the PS2, leading many a gamer to hold off on Sega's new console. (The fact that Sega bailed quickly on the Sega CD, 32X and Saturn didn't help with consumer confidence.) And the GameCube and original Xbox didn't launch until late 2001, after the PS2 had already gained a foothold.

To top it off, the PS2 stayed on the market for ages. Sony didn't stop making it until 2013, the same year its "grandchild," the PS4, launched. The PS2 also benefited from price cuts, dropping from $299 at its US launch to $199 in 2002, and eventually to $99 in 2009. By then, it was an attractive option for people who wanted a still-capable gaming system on the cheap.

All those hardware sales led to staggering software numbers: more than 1.5 billion PS2 games sold. Its best-selling titles were Grand Theft Auto: San Andreas at 17.3 million units, Gran Turismo 3: A-Spec at 14.9 million, and Grand Theft Auto: Vice City at 14.2 million.

Kicking off the era of the hybrid console, the Switch launched in 2017 with a novel concept: a mobile gaming device that you could also connect to a TV. It didn't take long to see Nintendo had a winner on its hands after the Wii U's disappointment. Helping out was the fact that its flagship launch game was an open-world classic, with The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild making the Switch's proposition instantly clear.

The result? Nintendo has shipped over 156 million Switch consoles as of June 2026, including all variants. The original model was followed by the cheaper and portable-only Switch Lite and the higher-end Switch OLED, which added a bigger and better screen.

The Switch has already beaten the PS2 in game sales, at nearly 1.6 billion units. Its top-selling titles are Mario Kart 8 Deluxe at 71.5 million units, Animal Crossing: New Horizons at 50.3 million and Super Smash Bros. Ultimate at 38.1 million.

Nintendo has said it expects to ship around 2 million original Switch consoles in the current fiscal year, which ends in March 2027. If that rate holds, the Switch could pass the PS2 in 2028 at the earliest.

The best-selling fully portable console was the Nintendo DS, which arrived in 2004. The system had a series of features that were novel at the time: two screens, a stylus, a built-in microphone and wireless play. Nintendo ended up shipping over 154 million units across all DS models.

The first versions were backward compatible with the Game Boy Advance's library, which likely helped with early sales. Later hardware iterations included the slimmer and lighter DS Lite, the DSi (which added two cameras, an SD card slot and a digital shop) and the larger-screened DSi XL.

The DS, which sold about 949 million games, was known as much for quirky titles — like Nintendogs and Brain Age which took advantage of the unique hardware — as it was for its tentpole franchises. The platform's best-selling games were New Super Mario Bros. at 30.8 million units, Nintendogs at 24 million and Mario Kart DS at 23.6 million.

The original Game Boy was an exercise in restraint. Rivals like the Atari Lynx and Sega Game Gear had color screens, but they cost more and ate up batteries at a faster clip. The Game Boy had a greenish screen, was relatively cheap and durable and ran for hours on four AA batteries. In North America and Europe, it also came with the ultimate pick-up-and-play game, Tetris.

Like most Nintendo consoles, the Game Boy had several iterations. The Game Boy Pocket arrived in 1996, with a smaller, lighter build and a sharper black-and-white screen. The Japan-only Game Boy Light added a backlight to the Pocket.

But the most notable hardware update was the Game Boy Color, which Nintendo lumps together with the original model in lifetime totals. It arrived nearly a decade after the original in 1998, soon after Pokémon took off, adding a full-color screen and compatibility with most older Game Boy games. Together, the Game Boy family sold over 118 million consoles.

The Game Boy family's top-selling games illustrate the console's pocketable appeal. On top was Pokémon Red, Green and Blue at 31.4 million units, followed by Tetris at 30.3 million and Pokémon Gold and Silver at 23.1 million.

The PS4's early success arguably marked the beginning of the end of the PlayStation-Xbox rivalry. Microsoft's initial Xbox One pitch, which included a mandatory daily online check-in and a mandatory Kinect camera, drew near-universal scorn. Although Microsoft reversed most of its plans before launch, the damage was done — and Sony was all too happy to reap the benefits, to the tune of 117 million PS4s shipped.

Sony learned from its mistakes with the PS3. The PS4 was much easier on developers, with a PC-esque architecture from AMD. Sony also continued to build on its growing reputation for first-party exclusives, including The Last of Us Part II and Uncharted 4: A Thief's End. The console got two hardware revisions: a slimmer model (known unofficially as the PS4 Slim) and the PS4 Pro, which added 4K support.

The system's best-selling games were Grand Theft Auto V at an estimated 24.7 million units, Marvel's Spider-Man at 22.7 million and God of War (2018) at 21 million.

Rounding out the top 10 are the original PlayStation at 102.4 million units shipped, the Nintendo Wii at 101.6 million, the PS5 at over 95 million (and counting) the PS3 at 87.4 million and the Xbox 360 (Microsoft's only entry on the list) at an estimated 84 million.

One of the reasons the PS2's record has held up is that the industry changed. The gaming business is much bigger today, but mobile gaming, PCs and subscription services now fight for a piece of the pie. That makes it harder for any single system to rack up PS2-like numbers. And current-gen hardware tends to get more expensive, not cheaper — especially in the age of RAMageddon. Good luck finding a current console for the PS2's final $99 price, or even the roughly $150 that works out to today, adjusting for inflation.

The OG Switch could take the crown within the next two or three years, assuming Nintendo doesn't discontinue it before it crosses the line. But until then, the 26-year-old console that made its bones spinning The Matrix DVDs stays on its perch.

Sarina Wiegman receives honorary damehood from Prince William at Windsor Castle - after leading Lionesses to back-to-back European titles

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Published: 14:28 EST, 10 November 2026 | Updated: 14:32 EST, 10 November 2026

Lionesses manager Sarina Wiegman has been awarded an honorary damehood by Prince William after leading England to back-to-back Euro wins.

Wiegman is Dutch and therefore cannot be made a substantive dame. Instead she has picked up the honorary title, because the Netherlands does not have a British monarch as its head of state.

She said upon being awarded the title at Windsor Castle on Tuesday: 'I feel very privileged to receive my honorary Damehood from His Royal Highness The Prince of Wales at Windsor Castle today.

'It is a day I will never forget and made even more special by sharing it with members of my family.

'I would like to again express my sincere gratitude for this honour and for the continued respect and warmth I experience from the English people. I thank everyone for their support.'

Wiegman was named in the King's New Year's Honours list at the end of 2025, alongside several members of her England team.

England manager Sarina Wiegman (left) has been awarded an honourary damehood by Prince William (right)

Captain Leah Williamson was awarded a CBE, with Alex Greenwood, Keira Walsh, Georgia Stanway and Ella Toone all being honoured with MBEs.

Wiegman said at the time: 'I would like to express my sincere gratitude for this honour. When I first arrived in England, I could never have imagined the respect and warmth I've experienced from the English people. I deeply thank the fans for their support.

'This title is a reflection of my exceptional team of players and staff. My congratulations go to Alex, Ella, Georgia, Keira, Leah and also Dr Ritan Mehta who have all been celebrated for their brilliant contributions.

'Our team values mean that when there is recognition for one, then there is recognition for all. I want to pay tribute to every member of our EURO squad. They should all be celebrated for everything they brought to the pitch and for the positive role they play in society. I'm proud to work alongside such a dedicated group of people.'

The former Netherlands coach took over the England team in 2021 and led them to success at Euro 2022 and Euro 2025.

She also reached the World Cup final in 2023, and signed a new two-year contract extension earlier this year, which will take her to the end of the 2029 Euros.

Rio Ngumoha explains why he left Chelsea for Liverpool: 'No-brainer'

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Ngumoha left Chelsea’s world-renowned academy to join Liverpool as a teenager

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Rio Ngumoha has revealed why he chose to leave Chelsea and move to Liverpool, branding the decision a “no-brainer”.

The teenage winger joined Chelsea’s academy - world-renowned for its youth development - at the age of eight and became one of the club’s most-prized starlets, with academy coaches previously stating that they believed Ngumoha could win the Ballon d’Or.

But at 16, Ngumoha left Chelsea and joined Liverpool, who oversaw the last of his development in junior ranks before watching him burst onto the scene at senior level.

Chelsea were disgruntled with the nature of Ngumoha’s exit and Liverpool were told in February to pay an initial £2.8m in compensation by a tribunal, with potential for that sum to rise to £6.8m with various add-ons.

Nevertheless, with Ngumoha now a regular under Liverpool boss Andoni Iraola after a breakout season last term, the 18-year-old has explained the reasoning behind his transfer decision and asserts he has no regrets over the switch.

“I feel like I needed a new challenge,” he said. “I feel like if I stayed at Chelsea... not saying that I wouldn't have got to the first team as quick, because I believe in my ability, but I feel like Liverpool just gave me a new pathway.

“The plan that they set for me was straightforward really. For me, it was a no-brainer going to Liverpool, and they just said if I prove myself, because they know how much ability I had, then they know that I would definitely be a first-team player and definitely a star for them in the making.

“I think going to Liverpool so far has been the right choice.”

While Ngumoha only crossed paths with the senior Chelsea team a handful of times during his time at Cobham, he reflects on an interaction he had with Raheem Sterling - who was similarly once a teenage prodigy - when training with the seniors.

“I trained with them only a few times but Raheem Sterling pulled me to the side a couple of times and told me he could see I had talent but I should never zone out in the game and I should always be in the game,” he said.

“The best players are always in the game in the last minutes. If you look at him and Mo [Salah], they’re always there until the crucial moments. Learning off them was important.”

Ngumoha has featured 14 times for Liverpool so far this season across all competitions, notching two assists which both came in a standout Champions League performance in the 4-3 win over Villarreal.

He was hailed by pundits and fans alike after that match, with Liverpool supporters chanting his name as he was substituted.

“Like I always say, Liverpool, we have the best fans in the world, especially Anfield on Champions League nights against big teams,” Ngumoha added.

“The way it roars, I've never heard anything like it. It's special. It's definitely special to play in front of them.”

Ngumoha earned his second call-up to the England squad for the November international break, again entrusted by Thomas Tuchel as the youngest player in his roster.

He could make his first England start in the coming days as the Three Lions see out their Nations League group-stage campaign, first with the visit of Croatia on Thursday before travelling to Madrid to face World Cup winners Spain on Saturday.

The latter will present him the opportunity to share the pitch with another winger sensation in Lamine Yamal, who was pipped to the Ballon d’Or by England captain Harry Kane last month.

Asked about that prospect, Ngumoha said: “I think what he’s doing changed football for young player. He’s phenomenal. He’s one of the best in the world.

“Seeing what he can do so young inspires me and all the other young players in how we can just play freely. The way he plays, it’s like it’s his pitch. It’s a good challenge.”
